# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_82828118.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 11, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. 
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda).  Upon request by Mr. Cook, and pursuant to support received by 
Mr. White and Mr. Johnson, items #17 & 18 were removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon 
request by Dr. Aldrich, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Cook, item 
#24(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Esquibel moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 26, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for about 923.93 acres of land located 
south of I-80 adjacent to Roundtop Road.  Comment was made by the following member of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich. There being no further comment or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending certain sections of Chapter 44, Solid Waste, 
and Chapter 56, Noise Control, of Title 8, Health and Safety, and further amending certain sections 
of Chapter 8, Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, and Chapter 12, Offenses Against 
Property, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, to eliminate the potential imposition of a jail sentence for violation of specified code 
provisions. Reporting for Finance Committee, Dr. Aldrich advised there was no recommendation 
from committee. Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White. 
Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by substitute dated February 27, 2024, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Dr. 
Rinne moved to amend by striking “shall” and adding “should to the maximum extent practical” 
to #6 on page 5, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by striking “and sanitary” in lines 2 & 3 in 
#4 on page 4, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion 
as amended by substitute, as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 

body.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. 
Johnson and Dr. Rinne. (#4553) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Creating Section 2.40.100, Public Defender and 
Representation of Needy Persons; Determination of Need, of Chapter 2.40, Municipal Court, of 
Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
Specifying Public Defender Appointment Procedures and Standards for Representation in the 
Municipal Court.  Dr. Aldrich advised there was no recommendation from Finance Committee. 
Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third reading, seconded by Mr. White. Dr. Aldrich moved to 
amend by substitute dated February 27, 2024, seconded by Mr. White. Motion to amend by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried. Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Comments were made by 
the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson. (#4554) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 10.12.160, Authority to Impound 
Illegally Parked Vehicles, and Section 10.12.170, Immobilization, of Chapter 10.12, 
Administration and Enforcement, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, modifying specified enforcement provisions regarding vehicle 
impoundment.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Mayor Collins 
and Mr. Laybourn. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density for land located south of West 5th Street between 
Patton Avenue and Snyder Avenue – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. 
Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the I-25 and I-80 Interchange adjacent to Roundtop Rd. – referred to Public 
Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)    
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AG Agricultural, LR Low-Density Residential, 
MR Medium-Density Residential, HI Heavy Industrial, and CB Community Business for land 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne located southwest of the I-25 and I-80 Interchange adjacent to 
Roundtop Road – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)    
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification of AG Agricultural, LR Low-Density Residential, 
MR Medium-Density Residential, HI Heavy Industrial, and CB Community Business to AG 
Agricultural and LI Light Industrial for land located southwest of the I-25 and I-80 Interchange 
adjacent to Roundtop Rd. – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)  
 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving the Department of Energy Efficiency and Conservation Block 
Grant Program Grant for lighting upgrades for the Cheyenne Civic Center. Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6389) 
    
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the support of the Governing Body for City staff to review 
and rewrite the City’s Parking ordinances for the purpose of introducing Paid On-Street Parking 
in Downtown Cheyenne and directing City staff to submit the ordinance rewrite for Governing 
Body consideration.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6390) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the support of the Governing Body for an Action Plan to 
address deficiencies regarding sidewalks in the Downtown Development District.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6391) 
 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board in the application for 
and acceptance of Federal Assistance from the Federal Aviation Administration under the Airport 
Improvement Program to assist in the purchase of snow removal equipment.  Dr. Aldrich moved 
to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Public comment was made by: Tim Bradshaw, Director of Aviation, Cheyenne Regional 
Airport.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and 
Mr. Johnson. (#6392) 
  
 
 
  
RESOLUTION – Supporting the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board in the application for 
and acceptance of Federal Assistance from the Federal Aviation Administration under the Airport 
Improvement Program to assist in Cheyenne Regional Airport's Master Plan and Airport Layout 
Plan.  Dr. Aldrich moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6393) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2024 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16-4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor to execute an amendment to the Development 
Agreement between the Board of County Commissioners of Laramie County, Wyoming, the City 
of Cheyenne, and COLL-IND, LLC regarding the Niobrara Energy Park – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich)    
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for North 
Row, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at 4612 Van Buren Ave) – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Whitney Ranch Third & Fourth Filing, LLC 
for a temporary construction easement for the Dell Range/Van Buren Improvements Project (2019-
2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per consent agenda) carried (in an amount not to 
exceed $250.00). (#7924)    
 
 

[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Benjamin D. Ward and Kimberly Ann Ward 
for a temporary construction easement at 4608 James Drive for the Dell Range/Van Buren 
Improvements Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per consent agenda) 
carried (in an amount not to exceed $250.00). (#7925) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jeff Trefren and Katrina Crossland for a 
temporary construction easement at 5700 Dell Range Boulevard for the Dell Range/Van Buren 
Improvements Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per consent agenda) 
carried (in an amount not to exceed $250.00). (#7926) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jeff T. Trefren for a temporary construction 
easement at 5708 Dell Range Boulevard for the Dell Range/Van Buren Improvements Project 
(2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per consent agenda) carried (in an amount not 
to exceed $250.00). (#7927)    
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Braidon Myers and Heather Myers for a 
temporary construction easement at 5420 Dell Range Boulevard for the Dell Range/Van Buren 
Improvements Project (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per consent agenda) 
carried (in an amount not to exceed $250.00). (#7928) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, 
Memorial Hospital of Laramie County d/b/a Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, Volunteers of America, 
Healthworks, Community Action of Laramie County, Wyoming Department of Corrections, Laramie 
County Sheriff’s Office, District Attorney, Drug Court and Treatment Court Programs, Laramie County 
and the Office of the State Public Defender for the Laramie County Law Enforcement Assisted Diversion 
(LEAD) Program Policy Coordinating Group – referred to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-1-24 for an agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
& Aztec JV Limited Partnership, for the Dell Range/Van Buren Improvements Project (2019-2022 
Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund, American Rescue Plan Federal Recovery Funds and Developer Joint 
Projects).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $9,287,323.65). 
(#7929)     
 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-11-24 for an agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Mechanical Systems, Inc., for the Cheyenne Sanitation Transfer Station - Lift Station Installation 
Project (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$111,417.00). (#7930) 
 
 
Consideration of Bid #E-12-24 for multiple twenty (20) passenger buses for the City of 
Cheyenne Transit Program (Transit Program Funds).  Dr. Aldrich moved to accept the bid from 
Model 1 Commercial Vehicles, Inc., dba Creative Bus Sales, Inc., in an amount not to exceed 
$1,661,767.40, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
 
Renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, 
resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, special malt beverage and winery 
permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  

Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Public comment was made 
by: Mike Moser, Wyoming State Liquor Association.  Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson and Mr. 
Roybal. 
 
[CA] 
Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the June 6, 2024 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Summer Convention Business 
Meeting to be held in Pinedale, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee.          
                     
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 25, 2024 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Rebecca 
Andrew Ventures, Inc. d/b/a Mr. Jim’s Pizza, 4001 Laramie St., Unit A, Cheyenne, WY. 
 
Under other business: 
 
Mr. Esquibel noted the motion to adopt the following Resolution - Requesting that the 
Wyoming State Legislature consider legislation authorizing the sale of alcoholic beverages with 
an alcohol by volume of 5% at grocery stores and convenience stores throughout the state, failed 
at the February 26, 2024 City Council meeting.  Having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant 
to Chapter 2.04 of City Code, Mr. Esquibel moved to reconsider the vote taken on the resolution, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal.  Voting “no” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Segrave 
and Mr. White.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to March 25, 2024, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  
Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White voting “no”.  During public comment, Dr. Rinne 
called a point of order related to comments not being made by the public.  Public comment was 
made by: Mike Moser, Wyoming State Liquor Association.  Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. 
Rinne and Mr. Roybal. 
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under other business:  Dr. 
Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel and Mr. Johnson.  
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:13 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 Kristina F. Jones, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 

 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 20, 2024

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_65555438.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 10.12.160, AUTHORITY TO 
IMPOUND ILLEGALLY PARKED VEHICLES, AND SECTION 10.12.170, 
IMMOBILIZATION, OF CHAPTER 10.12, ADMINISTRATION AND 
ENFORCEMENT, OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC, OF THE 
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, 
MODIFYING SPECIFIED ENFORCEMENT PROVISIONS REGARDING 
VEHICLE IMPOUNDMENT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.12.160, Authority to Impound Illegally Parked Vehicles, and 
Section 10.12.170, Immobilization, of Chapter 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this 
ordinance. 
 
10.12.160 - Authority to impound illegally parked vehicles. 
 
 
A. Any vehicle found parked in any unauthorized place or in violation of this code or any 
other city ordinance may be towed by the police department in accordance with subsection 
(B) of this section, except that any located within the territorial jurisdiction of Cheyenne 
may be towed and impounded, or caused to be towed and impounded, by the police 
department subject to the following conditions:  
 
1. If a vehicle has been placed on the scofflaw list or any vehicle of a registered owner 
whose name has been placed on the scofflaw list, the vehicle may be towed and 
impounded; 
 
2. If a vehicle is found parked in an unauthorized place or in violation of this code or any 
other city ordinance, the vehicle may be towed and impounded; and 
 
3. If a vehicle which was previously immobilized pursuant to under Section 
10.12.170(B)(3) 10.12.170(C) of the city code and that has not been released within 
two (2) business days, the vehicle shall be towed by the police department in 
accordance with subsection (B) of this section. The owner of the vehicle shall pay all 
reasonable charges of towing and storage incurred in addition to any penalties imposed 
for violation of this code or any other city ordinance. and impounded. 
 
B. When Any vehicle that is towed and impounded by the police department as authorized by 
this section, or otherwise authorized under city code or laws of this state, members of the 
police department may tow or otherwise remove a vehicle from a street or highway to the 
nearest garage or other place of safety, or shall be taken to a garage designated by the police 
department, or otherwise designated by the city. The registered owner of any vehicle that 
is towed and impounded under this section shall pay all reasonable charges for towing and 
11:43 am, Feb 21, 2024

Page 2 of 6 
 
storage of the vehicle in addition to any penalties imposed for violation of this code or any 
other city ordinance. 
 
C. Whenever an officer removes the police department tows a vehicle from a street for 
impoundment as authorized in this section, and the officer knows or is able to ascertain 
from the vehicle registration records the name and address of the owner, the officer 
department shall immediately give or cause to be given notice in writing to the registered 
owner of the fact of vehicle as indicated on the vehicle's registration records. The notice 
shall advise the owner of the removal, the reasons therefore, for the removal and the place 
to where the vehicle has been removed towed and impounded. In the event the vehicle is 
stored in a public garage, a copy of the notice shall be given to the proprietor of the garage. 
 
D. Whenever an officer removes If the police department tows or impounds a vehicle from a 
street as authorized under this section and does not know and is not able to cannot ascertain 
the name of the registered owner, or for any other reason is unable to give the notice to the 
registered owner as hereinbefore provided, by this section, and in the event the vehicle is 
not returned to the owner within a period of three (3) days, then the officer police 
department shall immediately send or cause to be sent a written report of the removal by 
mail to the state department whose duty it is to register motor vehicles Wyoming 
department of transportation. The officer department shall file a copy of the written notice 
with the proprietor of any public garage in which the vehicle may be stored. Notice shall 
include a complete description of the vehicle, the date, time and place and the place from 
which removed, it was towed, the date, time and reasons for the removal, and the name of 
the garage or place where the vehicle is being stored. 
 
10.12.170 - Immobilization. Scofflaw list; immobilization or towing and impoundment. 
 
A. Definition. For the purposes of As used in this section, the word "immobilize" or 
"immobilization" means and includes the use of any device which may be attached or 
affixed to a vehicle and when so attached or affixed shall is intended to render the vehicle 
immovable. 
 
B. Scofflaw List. As frequently as practicable, the police department parking administration 
manager shall prepare and update the "scofflaw list," consisting which list shall consist of 
vehicles any vehicle, and the registered owner of any vehicle, with three (3) default 
judgments as a result of or more unpaid parking citations.  
 
1. Notice that a vehicle or the registered owner of a vehicle is listed has been placed on the 
scofflaw list shall be served in the following manner: 
 
a. The police department by parking administration manager shall give notice on that 
registered owner by first class mail, to the registered owner of each vehicle listed on the 
scofflaw list, stating that the vehicle is listed on the scofflaw list. which notice shall also 
include the following: 
 

Page 3 of 6 
 
2. 
The content of the notice that a vehicle is listed on the scofflaw list shall contain not less 
than the following information: 
 
a. 1. The date and the nature of each ticket overdue and unpaid citation, the amount due 
on each citation and the vehicle for which the department issued the citation; 
 
b. 2. The date of entry of default judgement on for final payment of each unpaid parking 
citation; 
 
c. 3. That a scofflaw list fee of five dollars ($5.00) shall be imposed to cover 
administrative costs; 
 
d. 4. The total amount currently due; 
 
e. 5. That the registered owner shall, no have not less than fifteen (15) days after the date 
of mailing, to respond to the notice by paying the total amount due; and 
 
f. 6. That if the vehicle owner registered owner fails to respond within the prescribed 
time period, the listed fifteen (15) days after the date of mailing and pay all unpaid 
parking citations, that any vehicle registered to the owner: 
 
a. shall Shall be subject to immediate immobilization; and or  
 
g. b. 
That if the vehicle is impounded pursuant to Section 10.12.160 of the city 
code, May summarily be towed and impounded under Section 10.12.160(A)(1) of 
this chapter and that the registered owner shall also be responsible for the 
reasonable associated costs. of towing and storage. 
 
3. C. 
A vehicle The vehicle of any registered owner remaining on the scofflaw list after 
the date indicated stated in the notice issued pursuant to as required under paragraph (B)(2) 
of this section, is regardless whether the specific vehicle is on the scofflaw list, is subject 
to immobilization, or to being towed and impounded, by the chief of police or his designee 
while that police department whenever the vehicle is located upon any public property. The 
person immobilizing the If a vehicle is towed and impounded, notice shall be provided in 
accordance with Section 10.12.160 of this chapter. If a vehicle is immobilized, the 
department shall leave under the windshield wiper or otherwise attach to the vehicle a an 
immobilization notice advising owner that: 
 
1. The owner that the subject vehicle has been immobilized by the city for due to the 
owner's failure to pay amounts due for three (3) or more adjudicated unpaid parking 
infractions; that citations; 
 
2. The release of the vehicle from immobilization may be obtained secured by paying all 
outstanding fines and late fees owed relating to all adjudicated violations, unpaid 
parking citations, plus all removal, any applicable towing, and storage charges and 
administrative fees; that  

Page 4 of 6 
 
 
3. Unless such payment is made within two (2) business days of the date of the 
immobilization notice, the vehicle shall be summarily towed and impounded; that  
 
4. It is unlawful for any person to remove or attempt to remove the immobilization device, 
to damage the immobilization device, or to move the vehicle with the immobilization 
device attached, unless authorized by the chief of police or an authorized agent of the 
city; and that  
 
5. The owner may seek an administrative review of the immobilization by submitting a 
request to the police department parking administration manager within ten (10) days 
of the release of the immobilization device. 
 
4. D. 
If the Upon payment of all outstanding fines, fees and charges by the registered 
owner or an agent of the owner pays the fines and fees, if any, and all towing and storage 
charges, if any, of any vehicle immobilized or impounded in accordance with this section, 
the police department parking administration manager shall remove such vehicle the 
registered owner and any listed vehicle of a registered owner from the scofflaw list and 
authorize its the release from immobilization of any immobilized or impounded vehicle 
not later than the next business day. 
 
5. E. 
If a Any vehicle immobilized pursuant to this section that is not released within two 
(2) business days, (which does not include including the day the vehicle was immobilized), 
the police chief shall require the vehicle to shall be removed or cause it to be removed and 
towed towed and impounded in accordance with the impoundment provisions of Section 
10.12.160 of the city code. this chapter. 
 
C. Authorized. When the police chief finds a vehicle parked in violation of the provisions of 
this code, such person may immobilize the vehicle or cause the same to be done if that 
vehicle appears on the scofflaw list. 
 
D. F. 
Alternate Procedure. The provisions of this This section are declared is intended to 
provide an alternative manner for the enforcement of the provisions of enforcement 
authority under this code pertaining to parking violations and shall not preclude 
impoundment of the a vehicle parked as otherwise authorized under Section 10.12.160 of 
the city code this chapter or any other method of enforcing the parking ordinances. 
 
E. G. 
Penalty. Any person found guilty of tampering who tampers with an immobilization 
device in an attempt to remove or destroy it, the device is subject to the general penalty 
provision under guilty of a misdemeanor and punishable as provided by Section 1.24.010 
of the city code. 
 
F. H. 
Post-Immobilization Post-Deprivation Review. The registered owner of any 
vehicle owner immobilized or impounded under this section may seek a post-deprivation 
review of the immobilization by submitting a written request to the police department 
parking administration manager within ten (10) days of the placement of the 

Page 5 of 6 
 
immobilization device and notice on the vehicle, as established by the date of the 
immobilization notice date. The police department required under subsection (C) of this 
section. The registered owner of any vehicle towed and impounded as authorized under 
this section may seek a post-deprivation review by submitting a written request to the 
police department parking administration manager within ten (10) days of date of the 
vehicle being towed. The request shall include an explanation by the owner of why the 
immobilization or tow was allegedly improper.  
 
I. Upon receiving the request for a post-deprivation review as provided under subsection (H) 
of this section, the parking administration manager shall provide the request for review to 
the municipal court judge. Upon timely receipt of such written request, the municipal court 
judge shall, within a reasonable time as established by the court, conduct a review on the 
issue of whether the immobilization was proper and shall issue a written decision setting 
forth the reasons on which the decision is based, provided, however, that any previously 
adjudicated parking infractions that formed the basis of the vehicle's scofflaw status shall 
not be subject to the review. The person seeking review shall have an opportunity to present 
evidence on his or her behalf in accordance with requirements established by the court. 
review the facts and circumstances that caused the police department to immobilize the 
vehicle or to tow and impound the vehicle, shall review any relevant documentation, and 
take into consideration the explanation provided by the owner on why the immobilization 
or impoundment was allegedly improper and issue his or her decision. Upon review, the 
parking administration manager may make any determination as necessary and proper to 
dispose of the case, including the release of the vehicle and the waiver of an applicable 
fines or fees. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM 
MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL 
TO 
NR-2 
NEIGHBORHOOD RESIDENTIAL – MEDIUM DENSITY FOR LAND 
LOCATED SOUTH OF WEST 5TH STREET BETWEEN PATTON 
AVENUE AND SNYDER AVENUE." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on March 4, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lots 2 through 76, Block 1, Cheyenne Terrace, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby changed to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density. 
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
MARCH 4, 2024
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-24-17: Cheyenne Terrace 2nd Filing – Zoning Map Amendment 
(zone change)
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 4.581 acres of land from 
MR Medium-density Residential to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – 
Medium Density
LOCATION:
South of W 5th St, between Patton Avenue and Snyder Avenue.
APPLICANT: 
AVI PC
AGENT:
AVI PC
OWNER:
Pinewood Village LLC
ZONING:
MR Medium-density Residential
FUTURE LAND USE:
Urban Residential
PURPOSE: 
To allow for Cottage Lot Development  
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-24-17 
 
 
March 4, 2023 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 2 through 76, Block 1, Cheyenne Terrace 2nd, Filing to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
x 
UDC Article 5 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density for roughly 4.581 acres of land. The land is currently vacant 
on the south side of the property and there is existing multifamily on the north side of the property. There is 
an associated plat with this zone change which would subdivide the south side of the property for Cottage 
Lot development. The plat application is PUDC-24-7. Specific Lot layout may change with review of the plat, 
but the substance of the request will stay the same.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development.  
 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Urban Residential 
MR Medium-density 
Residential 
Vacant / Multi-family 
North 
Urban Residential 
MR  
Multi-family 
South 
Urban Residential 
MR  
Multi-family 
East 
Urban Residential 
MR 
Residential 
West 
Urban Residential 
MR  
Residential 

UDC-24-17 
 
 
March 4, 2023 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential and the surrounding area 
is Urban Residential. According to the comprehensive plan the optimal City zones for the subject 
property’s future land use designation is any one of the residential zones, including NR-2. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, NR-2 zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
All Surrounding properties are zoned MR. MR and NR-2 zones are medium-density residential 
zones. The difference is that NR-2 zoning anticipates alley-loaded development, allows smaller 
lots, and has a higher design standard. NR-2 zoning is compatible with surrounding zoning.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The MR, as put forward in the UDC, is intended for larger-block, front-loaded, suburban style 
development patterns (like Saddle Ridge or The Pointe). NR-2 zoning is intended for smaller-
block, rear-loaded, compact development patterns (like what is found in the Avenues). The 
proposed development is for cottage lots built in a townhouse style which better fits in the NR-2 
zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in a pre-existing neighborhood. No changes to the street network 
are proposed. There will be a private street that will connect Patton Avenue and Snyder Avenue.  
 
Accesses will not be changed with the zone change.  
 
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the area. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The NR-2 zone is intended for a medium density and mix of dwelling types that promote a 
consistent neighborhood character, with a compact development pattern and highly connected 

UDC-24-17 
 
 
March 4, 2023 
street network. The existing neighborhood meets these objectives. The proposed project is a 
cottage lot development which proposes a more compact type of development. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The NR-2 zone allows a variety of residential uses (as does the current MR zone). The applicant 
intends to build a cottage lot style development which is permitted in the NR-2 zone.  Should 
this particular project not move forward with cottage lots, the NR-2 zoning provides other 
options for infill residential development. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Proposal meets comprehensive plan principles. The entire area should be this zone based on the 
physical form of the neighborhood 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
NR-2 zone at this location. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The NR-2 zone 
is appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, surrounding development 
patterns, and proposed development. 
 
 

UDC-24-17 
 
 
March 4, 2023 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MR to NR-2. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Differences in uses per zone

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN 
ORDINANCE 
ANNEXING 
TO 
THE 
CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, LAND LOCATED SOUTHWEST 
OF THE I-25 AND I-80 INTERCHANGE ADJACENT TO 
ROUNDTOP RD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
SWAN Ranch, LLC Microsoft Corp, Dyno Nobel Inc., owners of:  
 
A PARCEL OF LAND BEING SITUATED IN A PORTION OF SECTIONS 9, 
10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15 AND 16, TOWNSHIP 13 NORTH RANGE 67 WEST OF 
THE 6TH PRINCIPAL MERIDIAN, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING AND 
BEING 
MORE 
PARTICULARLY 
DESCRIBED 
AS 
FOLLOWS: 
COMMENCING AT THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF SAID SECTION 9; 
THENCE ALONG THE WEST LINE OF SAID SECTION 9 N00°11'50”E A 
DISTANCE OF 1318.25 FEET TO A POINT ON THE PARCEL OF LAND 
DESCRIBED IN THE RECORDS OF THE LARAMIE COUNTY CLERK AT 
BOOK 2857, PAGE 1701 AND BEING THE TRUE POINT OF BEGINNING; 
THENCE CONTINUING ALONG SAID PARCELS BOUNDARY AND SAID 
WEST LINE OF SECTION 9 N00°11'50”E A DISTANCE OF 1318.27 FEET 
TO THE WEST ONE-QUARTER CORNER OF SAID SECTION 9; THENCE 
N00°05'58”E A DISTANCE OF 2294.10 FEET TO A POINT ON THE SOUTH 
RIGHT OF WAY OF INTERSTATE 80 AND THE BEGINNING OF A NON-
TANGENT CURVE TO THE RIGHT CONCAVE TO THE SOUTH; SAID 
CURVE HAVING A RADIUS OF 5529.60 FEET, A CHORD BEARING OF 
N89°09'39”E, A CHORD DISTANCE OF 55.83 FEET AND A CENTRAL 
ANGLE OF 00°34'42”, THENCE ALONG SAID SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY 
AND THE ARC OF SAID CURVE A DISTANCE OF 55.83 FEET; THENCE 
CONTINUING ALONG SAID SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY THE FOLLOWING 
COURSES AND DISTANCES: N89°29'59”E A DISTANCE OF 3020.56 FEET, 
THENCE S76°15'38”E A DISTANCE OF 939.31 FEET; THENCE N89°29'58”E

CONCAVE SOUTHEAST; SAID CURVE HAVING A RADIUS OF 602.58 
FEET, A CHORD BEARING OF N62°38'30”E, A CHORD DISTANCE OF 
107.64 FEET, AND A CENTRAL ANGLE OF 10°14'56” THENCE ALONG 
THE ARC OF SAID CURVE A DISTANCE OF 107.79 FEET TO THE 
BEGINNING OF A NON-TANGENT CURVE TO THE RIGHT CONCAVE 
SOUTHEAST; SAID CURVE HAVING A RADIUS OF 1215.16 FEET, A 
CHORD BEARING OF N64°06'53”E A CHORD DISTANCE OF 171.71 FEET 
AND A CENTRAL ANGLE OF 08°06'10” THENCE ALONG THE ARC OF 
SAID CURVE A DISTANCE OF 171.85 FEET; THENCE LEAVING SAID 
UPRR RIGHT OF WAY N04°22'40”E A DISTANCE OF 1066.80 FEET TO A 
POINT ON THE NORTH RIGHT OF WAY OF OTTO ROAD; THENCE 
N00°10'40”E A DISTANCE OF 1259.67 FEET; THENCE S89°28'58”W A 
DISTANCE OF 3205.77 FEET TO THE POINT OF BEGINNING. SAID 
PARCEL OF LAND CONTAINS 923.93 ACRES MORE OR LESS 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; 
 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Scott Bruce, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 16571; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical, and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned A-2 
Agricultural and Overland Trails PUD by the Laramie County Commissioners; 
and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 

its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph, or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs, and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

N62°38'30”E, A CHORD DISTANCE OF 107.64 FEET, AND A CENTRAL ANGLE OF 
10°14'56” THENCE ALONG THE ARC OF SAID CURVE A DISTANCE OF 107.79 
FEET TO THE BEGINNING OF A NON-TANGENT CURVE TO THE RIGHT 
CONCAVE SOUTHEAST; SAID CURVE HAVING A RADIUS OF 1215.16 FEET, A 
CHORD BEARING OF N64°06'53”E A CHORD DISTANCE OF 171.71 FEET AND A 
CENTRAL ANGLE OF 08°06'10” THENCE ALONG THE ARC OF SAID CURVE A 
DISTANCE OF 171.85 FEET; THENCE LEAVING SAID UPRR RIGHT OF WAY 
N04°22'40”E A DISTANCE OF 1066.80 FEET TO A POINT ON THE NORTH RIGHT 
OF WAY OF OTTO ROAD; THENCE N00°10'40”E A DISTANCE OF 1259.67 FEET; 
THENCE S89°28'58”W A DISTANCE OF 3205.77 FEET TO THE POINT OF 
BEGINNING. SAID PARCEL OF LAND CONTAINS 923.93 ACRES MORE OR LESS 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property described 
herein and to make it a part thereof. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on March 4, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as AG 
Agricultural for areas zoned A-2 Agricultural in the County, HI Heavy Industrial for lands zoned 
Overland Trails County PUD I, CB Community Business for lands zoned Overland Trails County 
PUD B, LR Low-density Residential for lands zoned Overland Trails County PUD R-L, MR 
Medium-density Residential for lands zoned Overland Trails County PUD R-M, and AG 
Agricultural for lands zoned Overland Trails County PUD OS. 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 

 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

OF WYOMING, DO HEREBY STATE THAT THIS MAP OF SURVEY WAS PREPARED
FROM FIELD NOTES TAKEN DURING AN ACTUAL SURVEY MADE BY ME OR UNDER

1. UPRR RIGHT-OF-WAY VARIES. UPRR RIGHT-OF-WAY BASED OFF HISTORICAL

WAS ACCEPTED AS THE RIGHT-OF-WAY BOUNDARY.

3. THIS ANNEXATION IS A METES AND BOUNDS LEGAL DESCRIPTION BASED
UPON THE SECTION CORNERS, PROPERTY CORNERS, AND FENCELINES
FOUND ON THE DATE FIELD WORK WAS COMPLETED.
LEGAL DESCRIPTION
A PARCEL OF LAND BEING SITUATED IN A PORTION OF SECTIONS 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15 AND 16, TOWNSHIP 13 NORTH RANGE
67 WEST OF THE 6TH PRINCIPAL MERIDIAN, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING AND BEING MORE PARTICULARLY DESCRIBED AS
FOLLOWS:
COMMENCING AT THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF SAID SECTION 9; THENCE ALONG THE WEST LINE OF SAID SECTION 9
N00°11'50”E A DISTANCE OF 1318.25 FEET TO A POINT ON THE PARCEL OF LAND DESCRIBED IN THE RECORDS OF THE
LARAMIE COUNTY CLERK AT BOOK 2857, PAGE 1701 AND BEING THE TRUE POINT OF BEGINNING; THENCE CONTINUING
ALONG SAID PARCELS BOUNDARY AND SAID WEST LINE OF SECTION 9 N00°11'50”E A DISTANCE OF 1318.27 FEET TO THE
WEST ONE-QUARTER CORNER OF SAID SECTION 9; THENCE N00°05'58”E A DISTANCE OF 2294.10 FEET TO A POINT ON THE
SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY OF INTERSTATE 80 AND THE BEGINNING OF A NON-TANGENT CURVE TO THE RIGHT CONCAVE TO THE
SOUTH; SAID CURVE HAVING A RADIUS OF 5529.60 FEET, A CHORD BEARING OF N89°09'39”E, A CHORD DISTANCE OF 55.83
FEET AND A CENTRAL ANGLE OF 00°34'42”, THENCE ALONG SAID SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY AND THE ARC OF SAID CURVE A
DISTANCE OF 55.83 FEET; THENCE CONTINUING ALONG SAID SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY THE FOLLOWING COURSES AND
DISTANCES:  N89°29'59”E A DISTANCE OF 3020.56 FEET, THENCE S76°15'38”E A DISTANCE OF 939.31 FEET; THENCE N89°29'58”E
A DISTANCE OF 1141.24 FEET TO A POINT OF INTERSECTION OF SAID SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY AND THE WEST RIGHT OF WAY
OF WYOMING HIGHWAY 222 (ROUNDTOP ROAD); THENCE ALONG SAID WEST RIGHT OF WAY THE FOLLOWING COURSES AND
DISTANCES:  S00°03'36”E A DISTANCE OF 846.53 FEET; THENCE S89°51'26”E A DISTANCE OF 27.71 FEET; THENCE S00°01'29”W A
DISTANCE OF 1214.81 FEET; THENCE S00°10'19”W A DISTANCE OF 1318.43 FEET;  THENCE S00°07'34”W A DISTANCE OF 298.54
FEET; TO A POINT OF INTERSECTION OF SAID WEST RIGHT OF WAY AND THE NORTH RIGHT OF WAY OF OTTO ROAD; THENCE
S07°05'04”E A DISTANCE OF 1253.07 FEET TO A POINT ON THE SOUTH RIGHT OF WAY OF THE UPRR; THENCE S00°34'22”W A
